# Knowledge Migration: Invite Link Platform

## Context & Details
This document serves as a knowledge migration log for the "Invite Link" platform project.

**Project Overview:**
- **Product:** A digital invitation creation platform for Event Planners and Printing Shops (B2B/B2B2C).
- **Tech Stack:** Next.js 16 (App Router), Tailwind CSS v4, GSAP, Framer Motion, React, Supabase (Auth), Vercel.
- **Key Features:**
  - Modern, glassmorphic UI with dynamic gradients and 3D perspectives.
  - Template Builder for Housewarming, Classic Wedding, Birthday Bash, Baby Shower.
  - Vercel Deployment via CLI under `invitationlink1@gmail.com`.
  - Analytics via GA4 & MS Clarity.
  - Contact/Lead Generation outreach (WhatsApp integration).
- **Accounts:**
  - `amigotechno21@gmail.com` (Original Vercel/GitHub scope)
  - `invitationlink1@gmail.com` (Target production Vercel/GitHub scope)

## ✨ What's Inside

### 4 Cinematic Screens

| Screen | Purpose | Background Image |
|--------|---------|-----------------|
| **1 — Open the Door** | Emotional hook with Bible verse, "Tap to Open" | Arched teak doorway |
| **2 — Invitation Details** | Date, time, venue + RSVP & Directions | Curtains & flowers |
| **3 — Memories** | 2×2 memory cards — future moments | Candle & floral scene |
| **4 — Thank You** | Emotional closure with hosts' names | Evening candlelight |

### Your Specific Requests ✅

- **Bible verse** (Psalm 91:1) — prominently placed at the top of Screen 1, right below the cross
- **Phone number** (7259751804) — displayed in the RSVP modal under Joylin & Noel Monis' name
- **"Leave a Blessing" removed** — completely stripped from the design (action cards now show only RSVP + Directions)

### Cinematic Interactions

- 🚪 **Door opening** — golden light expansion, slow fade, cinematic scale
- ✨ **Floating dust particles** — 18 per section, each with unique organic drift
- ☀️ **Moving sunlight** — pointer-reactive light beams
- 🕯️ **Candle flicker** — natural imperfect flame animation on Screen 4
- 📜 **Scroll reveals** — staggered fade-in-up with cinematic easing
- 🃏 **Memory cards** — Ken Burns zoom effect + gentle floating
- 📱 **RSVP modal** — bottom-sheet with swipe-to-close gesture
- 🎯 **Nav dots** — fixed sidebar for section jumping

### Design Language

Drawn directly from your reference mockups: deep **maroon** (#6B2036), warm **gold** (#C4A35A), **ivory** backgrounds, **Playfair Display** + **Cormorant Garamond** serifs, **Inter** body text, **Dancing Script** for handwritten moments.

> **Tip**: View it on your phone (or use Chrome DevTools mobile preview, `Ctrl+Shift+M`) for the intended vertical experience.

25:

27:

31: 2. **Technical Correctness**

33:

38:    No random decoration. Every flourish must serve the aesthetic thesis.
39:

---

## What's Fixed & Improved

### 🔤 Visibility (was broken)
| Element | Before | After |
|---------|--------|-------|
| **"Tap to Open"** | Tiny gold text, invisible on image | **Bold maroon pill button** with frosted glass background + gold border |
| **Bible verse** | Light secondary color, blended in | **Primary color + text-shadow** for ivory glow behind text |
| **"Welcome to"** | Small muted text | **Bolder weight + text-shadow** |
| **NORAH title** | Thin weight, no halo | **Font-weight 600 + ivory glow shadow** |
| **Door subtitle** | Secondary color | **Primary color + shadow** |
| **Door quote** | Faint italic | **Bolder + shadow** |
| **All overlays** | 45-72% opacity (too transparent) | **55-92% opacity** — text areas now have strong ivory wash |

### 🎬 Screens (was 4, now 3)
- ❌ **Removed**: Memories screen (filler AI images)
- ✅ **Screen 1**: Door — Bible verse, cross, NORAH, tap to open
- ✅ **Screen 2**: Invitation — hosts, details, RSVP + directions
- ✅ **Screen 3**: Closing — thank you, prayer blessing, Numbers 6:24-25 scripture, hosts, CTA

### ✨ Interactions Added
- **Haptic vibration** on door tap, RSVP, and See You button (mobile)
- **Golden petal burst** (24 petals) when door opens
- **Heart confetti** (❤️) when selecting "Gladly attending"
- **Device tilt parallax** — sunlight beams respond to phone tilting
- **Cross appears in transition** — golden cross fades in during door-to-invitation wipe

### 🙏 Soul Added
- **Prayer card** with blessing: "May the Lord bless this home and all who enter it..."
- **Closing scripture**: Numbers 6:24-25 — "The Lord bless you and keep you..."
- **Glowing cross** with pulsing ring at the top of Door and Closing screens
- **"Amen"** in the prayer card with editorial styling

> **To test on mobile**: Press `F12` → toggle device toolbar (`Ctrl+Shift+M`) → select a phone → tap the door.

### 🤖 Assistant:

Here's my brainstorm for the NORAH invitation — things that could elevate it further and things that might be trimmed for a cleaner experience:

---

## ✨ Could Add

| Idea | Why |
|------|-----|
| **Countdown Timer** | A live countdown to July 25th on the invitation screen — builds anticipation |
| **Photo Gallery / Carousel** | 3-4 swipeable photos of the new home (exterior, living room, balcony view) — makes it personal |
| **"Add to Calendar" Button** | One-tap Google/Apple calendar event with date, time & venue auto-filled |
| **Background Music Controls** | A small "Now Playing" label showing the track name near the audio toggle |
| **Confetti/Petal Animation** on RSVP tap | A burst of petals when someone confirms attendance — delightful micro-interaction |
| **Family Photo** | A small, warm photo of Joylin, Noel & family on the Thank You screen |
| **Share Button** | Let guests forward the invitation link via WhatsApp/copy-link |
| **Guest Counter** | "X families have RSVP'd" — adds social proof and excitement |
| **Map Preview** | An embedded mini-map or a static map image of the venue location |
| **Dress Code Hint** | A subtle line like *"Dress Code: Smart Casual / Traditional"* |

---

## ✂️ Could Remove / Simplify

| Idea | Why |
|------|-----|
| **"A home built with prayers, love & grace"** on Screen 2 | Feels redundant since the Bible verse already sets that tone |
| **Film grain texture overlay** | Very subtle — most users won't notice it, but it adds rendering cost |
| **Particles / floating dust** | Pretty but can feel noisy on slower phones — could simplify to just the sunlight beam |
| **Handwritten "Can't wait to make memories"** | Overlaps with the "See You Soon" sentiment — one or the other might be enough |
| **Double quote blocks** on Thank You screen | "A house is made of walls..." AND "A home is made of moments..." — pick the stronger one |
| **Gold dividers** | There are several `♦` and `♥` dividers — reducing to fewer keeps it more minimal |

---

## 🔥 High-Impact Quick Wins

1. **Add to Calendar** — guests will genuinely use this
2. **Countdown Timer** — instant "wow" factor, very on-brand
3. **Share via WhatsApp** button — makes forwarding effortless
4. **Remove one of the two closing quotes** — tighter = more elegant

Want me to implement any of these?

| **⏱ Countdown Timer** | Live countdown to July 25, 2026 at 12:00 PM — shows Days, Hours, Mins, Secs in elegant maroon boxes with gold borders |
| **📅 Add to Calendar** | Gold-bordered button below RSVP/Directions — downloads a `.ics` file that works with Google Calendar, Apple Calendar, Outlook |
| **🎉 Confetti Burst** | 60-piece colorful confetti explosion (maroon, gold, ivory) triggers on RSVP "Gladly attending" and on "See You Soon" button tap |

| **Floating particles** (all 3 screens) | Cleaner look, better performance on mobile |
| **Handwritten "Can't wait to make memories"** | Overlapped with "See You Soon" sentiment |
| **"A house is made of walls and beams"** quote | Kept only the stronger "A home is made of moments and people" |

1: # NORAH — Customizable Invitation Template Platform
2: 
3: Turn the existing NORAH housewarming invitation into a reusable template that anyone can customize via a builder UI, then share via a unique generated URL.
4: 
5: ## Architecture Overview
6: 
7: ```mermaid
8: flowchart LR
9:     A[Builder Page<br>/builder] -->|Saves data + images| B[Supabase<br>Database + Storage]
10:     B -->|Returns invite_id| A
11:     A -->|Generates link| C[norah-templates.vercel.app<br>/invite/abc123]
12:     C -->|Fetches data| B
13:     C -->|Renders| D[Personalized Invitation]
14: ```
15: 
16: ## Tech Stack
17: 
18: | Layer | Technology | Why |
19: |-------|-----------|-----|
20: | **Frontend** | Vite + Vanilla JS | Fast, lightweight, same tech as current NORAH |
21: | **Backend** | Vercel Serverless Functions (API Routes) | Zero-config, free tier, same hosting |
22: | **Database** | Supabase (PostgreSQL) | Free tier, real-time, built-in auth & storage |
23: | **Image Storage** | Supabase Storage | Upload custom background photos |
24: | **Hosting** | Vercel | Already in use |
25: 
26: > [!IMPORTANT]
27: > This will be a **new separate project** deployed to a different URL (e.g. `norah-templates.vercel.app`). The original NORAH invitation stays untouched at its current URL.
28: 
29: ## Customizable Fields
30: 
31: ### Core Details
32: - **Home name** (e.g., "NORAH") — the main title
33: - **Hosts** (e.g., "Joylin & Noel Monis")
34: - **Date** (day, month, year)
35: - **Time** (start time + optional end time)
36: - **Venue** (name, address, G
<truncated 4375 bytes>
: "/invite/:slug", "destination": "/invite.html" },
156:     { "source": "/api/create", "destination": "/api/create.js" },
157:     { "source": "/api/get", "destination": "/api/get.js" }
158:   ]
159: }
160: ```
161: 
162: ---
163: 
164: ## User Flow
165: 
166: ```mermaid
167: flowchart TD
168:     A[User visits /builder] --> B[Fills in details, uploads images]
169:     B --> C[Clicks 'Preview']
170:     C --> D[Sees live preview of invitation]
171:     D --> E[Clicks 'Generate Link']
172:     E --> F[API saves to Supabase]
173:     F --> G[Returns unique slug]
174:     G --> H["Shows shareable link:<br>norah-templates.vercel.app/invite/abc123"]
175:     H --> I[User shares via WhatsApp/copy]
176:     I --> J[Guest opens link]
177:     J --> K[Template fetches data from Supabase]
178:     K --> L[Renders personalized invitation]
179: ```
180: 
181: ---

I have built and deployed the dynamic invitation platform for you!

Here are the details:

*   **Live Builder Dashboard**: [https://norah-templates.vercel.app](https://norah-templates.vercel.app)
*   **GitHub Repository**: [https://github.com/invitation-link/norah-templates](https://github.com/invitation-link/norah-templates)

### What is Completed:
1.  **Database & Storage (Supabase)**: Created the `invitations` database table and a public storage bucket for image/music uploads. Added row-level policies so anyone can build cards but cannot edit other people's data.
2.  **Builder Dashboard (`index.html`)**: Features a step-by-step editor (Details, Content, Design, Publish) and a live mobile preview frame that updates instantly as you type.
3.  **Dynamic Template Renderer (`invite.html`)**: Rebuilt the original cinematic code to load database configurations on the fly based on the URL path (e.g. `/invite/slug`). All original animations, colors, and music are fully supported.
4.  **Auto-deployment (Vercel)**: Pushed to GitHub and deployed live.

For a full breakdown, please refer to the [walkthrough.md](file:///C:/Users/venka/.gemini/antigravity/brain/3909f982-2586-45bb-8143-e99dd361dc1e/walkthrough.md) file. Try creating your first customized invitation card on the dashboard!

The following code has been modified to include a line number before every line, in the format: <line_number>: <original_line>. Please note that any changes targeting the original code should remove the line number, colon, and leading space.
1: # Norah Templates Platform Walkthrough
2: 
3: I have built, configured, and deployed the **Norah Housewarming Invitation Template Platform**! 
4: 
5: ## Live Links
6: 
7: *   **Builder Dashboard**: [https://norah-templates.vercel.app](https://norah-templates.vercel.app)
8: *   **GitHub Repository**: [https://github.com/invitation-link/norah-templates](https://github.com/invitation-link/norah-templates)
9: *   **Database Panel**: Connected to your Supabase project under account `invitationlink1@gmail.com`.
10: 
11: ---
12: 
13: ## What Was Built
14: 
15: We created a brand new, fully static, serverless invitation platform:
16: 
17: ### 1. The Builder Dashboard (`index.html`)
18: *   **Step-by-Step Editor**: Categorized into Event Details, Content/Scripture, Design/Theming, and Share/Publish.
19: *   **Live Interactive Mobile Preview**: An embedded mobile phone mock-up on the right panel updates instantly as you type via `postMessage`.
20: *   **Supabase Storage Uploads**: Drag-and-drop or select local images/music files. The client automatically uploads them to your public Supabase Storage bucket and stores the public URLs.
21: *   **Link Generation**: Checks URL availability in real time, saves the configuration to PostgreSQL, and outputs a one-click copyable URL + WhatsApp share button.
22: 
23: ### 2. The Dynamic Template Renderer (`invite.html`)
24: *   Refactored the original cinematic housewarming animation code to be completely data-driven.
25: *   Reads the URL path (e.g., `/invite/your-slug`) and fetches the corresponding layout variables, colors, texts, background assets, and music from Supabase.
26: *   Retains the luxury animations: cinematic doors opening, falling gold petals, heart confetti on RSVPs, scroll-based fade-in reveals, and audio controls.
27: 
28: ### 3. Backend & Database Setup (Supabase)
29: *   **Table `invitations`**: Stores all customizable texts, date/time values, custom HEX colors, and uploaded asset URLs.
30: *   **Row-Level Security (RLS)**: Configured public `SELECT` and `INSERT` policies so anyone can create and view invitations, but nobody can update or delete someone else's invitation.
31: *   **Bucket `invitations`**: Publicly accessible storage bucket created for uploading background images and music assets.
32: 
33: ---
34: 
35: ## How to Test
36: 
37: 1. Open the [Builder Dashboard](https://norah-templates.vercel.app).
38: 2. Enter your custom text (e.g., Change Home Name to "VILLA GRACE", update hosts, maps coordinates, and select a preset background or upload your own files).
39: 3. Watch the mobile preview on the right update in real time.
40: 4. Go to **Step 4 (Share)**, pick a custom link slug (e.g., `test-drive`), and click **Generate Invitation Link**.
41: 5. Once published, click **View Live Card** or copy the link to test it on your mobile device!
42: 
The above content shows the entire, complete file contents of the requested file.

### 🤖 Assistant:

I have successfully implemented the interactive editing functionality and deployed it live!

### How It Works:
1. **Interactive Preview Editing**: You can now edit text **directly in the mobile preview** on the right! Simply tap or click on any text inside the phone mockup, start typing, and the form fields on the left will update dynamically in real time.
2. **Dynamic Syncing**: The builder uses smart syncing to avoid resetting the typing focus/cursor in the preview screen while simultaneously updating all occurrences of that text across other screens.
3. **Pushed & Live**: Committed and deployed to Vercel: **[https://norah-templates.vercel.app](https://norah-templates.vercel.app)**.

+    const door = document.getElementById('screen-door');
+    const invite = document.getElementById('screen-invitation');

+
+    if (screen === 'door') {
+      door.classList.remove('screen--hidden');
+      door.style.opacity = '1';

+      door.classList.add('screen--hidden');
+      invite.classList.remove('screen--hidden');
+      closing.classList.add('screen--hidden');
+      triggerReveals(invite);
+      window.scrollTo({ top: 0, behavior: 'instant' });

+      door.classList.add('screen--hidden');
+      invite.classList.add('screen--hidden');
+      closing.classList.remove('screen--hidden');

### 🤖 Assistant:

I have successfully implemented and pushed the **automatic screen-navigation** logic to the builder!

### How It Works Now:
1. **Auto-Transition on Step Navigation**:
   * When you are on **Step 1 (Details)** or **Step 2 (Content)**, the mobile preview automatically opens the door and displays the **Invitation Screen (Screen 2)**.
   * When you go to **Step 3 (Design)**, it shows the **Door Screen (Screen 1)** so you can preview the entry styles.
2. **Auto-Transition on Input Focus**:
   * As you click or tab into different input fields on the left, the preview **automatically slides/switches** to the exact screen where that text appears!
   * For example, focusing on the *Scripture/Quote* or *See You Soon Button* immediately slides the preview to the **Thank You Screen (Screen 3)**. Focusing on *Welcome Eyebrow* immediately slides back to the **Door Screen (Screen 1)**.
3. **Pushed & Live**: Committed and deployed to Vercel.

Give it a try by clicking into different fields on your live dashboard at **[https://norah-templates.vercel.app](https://norah-templates.vercel.app)**!

<USER_REQUEST>
I want you to build main website of invitation link 
from the following url and content 

# Invitation Link — Design Brief & Spec

**Version 1 · Phase 1 (microsite invitation card)**

---

## 1. The brand in one line

Invitation Link turns a static invitation into one shareable link that holds the whole event — RSVP, maps, schedule, updates — reducing coordination effort for hosts and confusion for guests. **The product is coordination, not decoration.**

Tagline: **INVITE · CONNECT · CELEBRATE**
Product line: *"Send the event. Not just the details."*

---

## 2. Logo concept — "The Open Door"

The chosen direction (from the explored options, the door-monogram in Image 8) is refined into an **open door built from the letters i + L**:

- Left jamb + floor = navy `i` stem and `L` base (structure, trust).
- The angled door panel = magenta→violet gradient (celebration + the connecting link).
- A small dot = the door handle, doubling as the `i` tittle.

Why this over the alternatives: it states the core promise visually (open one link, step into the event), it is **occasion-neutral** (no heart, so it serves corporate + baby shower + wedding alike), and the monogram reads at favicon size — critical because the icon is the WhatsApp share thumbnail, your main distribution surface.

**One ownability note for the designer:** the open-door motif is common in real-estate/access branding. Keep the `iL` letterform unmistakable inside the door so it reads as *our* monogram, not a generic icon.

### Files delivered
| File | Use |
|---|---|
| `logo-primary.svg/.png` | Light backgrounds, default |
| `logo-dark.svg/.png` | Dark backgrounds / footers |
| `logo-icon.svg/.png` | App icon, favicon, WhatsApp avatar |
| `logo-mono.svg/.png` | Print, stamps, single-color |
| `favicon.png` | 64px browser tab |

---

## 3. Color & type

**Primary:** Ink Navy `#1B1F3B` · Celebration Magenta `#E8447E` · Connection Violet `#7A3FF2`
**Neutrals:** Cream `#FAF8F5` · Dark BG

1: # NORAH — Customizable Invitation Template Platform
2: 
3: Turn the existing NORAH housewarming invitation into a reusable template that anyone can customize via a builder UI, then share via a unique generated URL.
4: 
5: ## Architecture Overview
6: 
7: ```mermaid
8: flowchart LR
9:     A[Builder Page<br>/builder] -->|Saves data + images| B[Supabase<br>Database + Storage]
10:     B -->|Returns invite_id| A
11:     A -->|Generates link| C[norah-templates.vercel.app<br>/invite/abc123]
12:     C -->|Fetches data| B
13:     C -->|Renders| D[Personalized Invitation]
14: ```
15: 
16: ## Tech Stack
17: 
18: | Layer | Technology | Why |
19: |-------|-----------|-----|
20: | **Frontend** | Vite + Vanilla JS | Fast, lightweight, same tech as current NORAH |
21: | **Backend** | Vercel Serverless Functions (API Routes) | Zero-config, free tier, same hosting |
22: | **Database** | Supabase (PostgreSQL) | Free tier, real-time, built-in auth & storage |
23: | **Image Storage** | Supabase Storage | Upload custom background photos |
24: | **Hosting** | Vercel | Already in use |
25: 
26: > [!IMPORTANT]
27: > This will be a **new separate project** deployed to a different URL (e.g. `norah-templates.vercel.app`). The original NORAH invitation stays untouched at its current URL.
28: 
29: ## Customizable Fields
30: 
31: ### Core Details
32: - **Home name** (e.g., "NORAH") — the main title
33: - **Hosts** (e.g., "Joylin & Noel Monis")
34: - **Date** (day, month, year)
35: - **Time** (start time + optional end time)
36: - **Venue** (name, address, G

158:   ]
159: }
160: ```
161: 
162: ---
163: 
164: ## User Flow
165: 
166: ```mermaid
167: flowchart TD
168:     A[User visits /builder] --> B[Fills in details, uploads images]
169:     B --> C[Clicks 'Preview']
170:     C --> D[Sees live preview of invitation]
171:     D --> E[Clicks 'Generate Link']
172:     E --> F[API saves to Supabase]
173:     F --> G[Returns unique slug]
174:     G --> H["Shows shareable link:<br>norah-templates.vercel.app/invite/abc123"]
175:     H --> I[User shares via WhatsApp/copy]
176:     I --> J[Guest opens link]
177:     J --> K[Template fetches data from Supabase]
178:     K --> L[Renders personalized invitation]
179: ```

2: name: token-optimiser

8: 
9: # Token Optimiser
10: 
11: Process huge volumes of research, PDFs, and code using a fraction of your plan's tokens — same output quality, far less consumed.
12: 
13: ## When to Use
14: 
15: Apply this skill **proactively** whenever:
16: 
17: - Working with large codebases, documents, or research corpora
18: - A session is getting long (50+ messages or approaching context limits)
19: - Processing multiple files, PDFs, or web pages in sequence
20: - Planning to use MCP servers or heavy tool integrations
21: - Running exploratory work before a focused implementation phase
22: - Noticing sluggish responses or degraded output quality (context rot symptoms)
23: - Working under a token budget or usage cap
24: 
25: **Do NOT apply** when:
26: - Quick one-shot questions with small context
27: - Debugging a single file where full context is essential
28: - Tasks that genuinely require the entire conversation history
29: 
30: ---
31: 
32: ## Strategy 1: Strategic Compaction
33: 
34: ### The Problem
35: Auto-compaction triggers at unpredictable times, often mid-thought, losing critical context. It compresses everything uniformly regardless of importance.
36: 
37: ### The Solution
38: Compact **manually** at logical breakpoints with a curated summary.
39: 
40: ### W

426: 
427: ## Quick Reference: Token Cost of Common Operations
428: 
429: | Operation | Approximate Tokens | Optimised Alternative | Savings |
430: |-----------|-------------------|----------------------|---------|
431: | Read entire 500-line file | 2,000 | Grep + line-range read | 80% |
432: | Load MCP server | 3,000-5,000 | Use CLI tool | 95% |
433: | Full `git log` | 2,000-5,000 | `git log -n 5 --oneline` | 90% |
434: | Read raw HTML page | 10,000-50,000 | Extract text/markdown | 85% |

437: | Research 10 web pages | 50,000-100,000 | Subagent + summaries | 90% |
438: | Process 200-page PDF | 100,000+ | Batch + summarize | 85% |
439: 
440: ## Anti-Patterns to Avoid
441: 
442: 1. **"Load Everything First"** — Don't read all project files before understanding the task
443: 2. **"MCP by Default"** — Don't reach for MCP servers when a CLI command works
444: 3. **"Explore in Main"** — Don't do research/exploration in the primary session
445: 4. **"Read to Remember"** — Don't re-read files you've already processed; save key info
446: 5. **"Ignore the Meter"** — Don't ignore context budget until auto-compaction forces it
447: 6. **"Verbose Summaries"** — Don't write paragraph summaries; use structured bullet points
448: 7. **"One Giant Session"** — Don't try to do everything in one session; break at milestones
449: 8. **"Raw Data Dumps"** — Don't paste raw JSON/HTML/logs; filter first, paste relevant parts
450:

8: # Find Skill
9: 
10: Describe any task in plain English and this skill automatically finds, ranks, and runs the right skill from the entire ecosystem.
11: 
12: ## When to Use
13: 
14: - The user says "find a skill for X" or "how do I do X" or "is there a skill for X"
15: - The user describes a task and you need to determine which installed skill handles it
16: - The user wants to browse or search the skill library
17: - You need to suggest a skill that isn't installed yet but would help
18: - The user asks "what skills do I have" or "list my skills"
19: - A task clearly maps to an existing skill but the user didn't invoke it by name
20: 
21: ## Instructions
22: 
23: ### Phase 1 — Understand the Task
24: 
25: 1. Parse the user's request into a clear **task intent**. Extract:
26:    - **Action**: What they want to do (build, debug, deploy, write, test, audit, etc.)
27:    - **Domain**: The subject area (frontend, backend, security, DevOps, AI/ML, marketing, etc.)
28:    - **Stack**: Any technologies mentioned (React, Python, AWS, Terraform, etc.)
29:    - **Specificity**: Is this a broad category or a precise tool/workflow?
30: 
31: 2. If the request is ambiguous, ask **one** clarifying question. Do not start an extended interview —
<truncated 8252 bytes>
rt patterns for Clerk auth implementation, middleware,
241:          organizations, webhooks, and user sync

9: # Vercel Deployment
10: 
11: Expert knowledge for deploying to Vercel with Next.js
12: 
13: ## Capabilities
14: 
15: - vercel
16: - deployment
17: - edge-functions
18: - serverless
19: - environment-variables
20: 
21: ## Prerequisites
22: 
23: - Required skills: nextjs-app-router
24: 
25: ## Patterns
26: 
27: ### Environment Variables Setup
28: 
29: Properly configure environment variables for all environments
30: 
31: **When to use**: Setting up a new project on Vercel
32: 
33: // Three environments in Vercel:
34: // - Development (local)
35: // - Preview (PR deployments)
36: // - Production (main branch)
37: 
38: // In Vercel Dashboard:
39: // Settings → Environment Variables
40: 
41: // PUBLIC variables (exposed to browser)
42: NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://xxx.supabase.co
43: NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=eyJ...
44: 
45: // PRIVATE variables (server only)
46: SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=eyJ...  // Never NEXT_PUBLIC_!
47: DATABASE_URL=postgresql://...
48: 
49: // Per-environment values:
50: // Production: Real database, production API keys
51: // Preview: Staging database, test API keys
52: // Development: Local/dev values (also in .env.local)
53: 
54: // In code, check environment:
55: const isProduction = process.env.VERCEL_ENV === 'production'
56: const isPreview = process.env.VERCEL_ENV === 'preview'

+### Phase 2: Template Gallery Integration (Current)
+
+**Goal**: Transform the main page (`index.html`) from a single-product pitch into a platform showcasing multiple event templates. 
+
+#### Proposed Changes
+
+1. **`index.html` [MODIFY]**:
+   - Insert a new **Templates Gallery** section immediately following the Hero section.
+   - Display a responsive grid of at least 4 template types:
+     - **Housewarming** (Existing)
+     - **Classic Wedding** (Elegant, floral, traditional)
+     - **Birthday Bash** (Vibrant, fun, celebratory)
+     - **Baby Shower** (Soft, pastel, warm)
+   - Each card will feature a premium generated thumbnail, title, description, and a "Use Template" CTA button linking to `/builder?template=[type]`.
+
+2. **`site.css` [MODIFY]**:
+   - Add CSS for the new `.template-grid` and `.template-card` components.
+   - Implement hover micro-animations (e.g., slight lift, shadow expansion, image zoom) to make the gallery feel dynamic and premium.
+
+3. **Image Generation**:
+   - Use the `generate_image` tool to create beautiful, custom template thumbnails for the Wedding, Birthday, and Baby Shower cards so we don't rely on placeholders.
+
+## User Review Required
+
+> [!IMPORTANT]

+/* Templates Gallery */
+.template-grid {
+  display: grid;

+  gap: 32px;
+  margin-top: 40px;
+}

+  background: white;
+  border-radius: var(--radius-lg);
+  overflow: hidden;
+  box-shadow: var(--shadow-sm);
+  transition: all 0.4s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1);

+  display: flex;
+  flex-direction: column;
+}
+.template-card:hover {
+  transform: translateY(-8px);
+  box-shadow: var(--shadow-lg);
+}
+.template-image-wrapper {
+  aspect-ratio: 4/3;
+  overflow: hidden;
+  position: relative;
+  background: var(--cream);
+}
+.template-image {
+  width: 100%;
+  height: 100%;
+  object-fit: cover;
+  transition: transform 0.5s ease;
+}
+.template-card:hover .template-image {
+  transform: scale(1.05);
+}
+.template-content {
+  padding: 24px;
+  display: flex;
+  flex-direction: column;
+  flex-grow: 1;
+}
+.template-content h3 {
+  margin-bottom: 8px;
+  font-size: 1.25rem;
+}
+.template-content p {
+  font-size: 0.95rem;
+  margin-bottom: 24px;
+  flex-grow: 1;
+}
+.template-card .btn {
+  width: 100%;
+}

8: # Find Skill

10: Describe any task in plain English and this skill automatically finds, ranks, and runs the right skill from the entire ecosystem.
11: 
12: ## When to Use
13: 
14: - The user says "find a skill for X" or "how do I do X" or "is there a skill for X"
15: - The user describes a task and you need to determine which installed skill handles it
16: - The user wants to browse or search the skill library
17: - You need to suggest a skill that isn't installed yet but would help
18: - The user asks "what skills do I have" or "list my skills"
19: - A task clearly maps to an existing skill but the user didn't invoke it by name
20: 
21: ## Instructions
22: 
23: ### Phase 1 — Understand the Task

25: 1. Parse the user's request into a clear **task intent**. Extract:

27:    - **Domain**: The subject area (frontend, backend, security, DevOps, AI/ML, marketing, etc.)
28:    - **Stack**: Any technologies mentioned (React, Python, AWS, Terraform, etc.)
29:    - **Specificity**: Is this a broad category or a precise tool/workflow?
30: 
31: 2. If the request is ambiguous, ask **one** clarifying question. Do not start an extended interview —

There are many invitation card printing shops in Hyderabad that cater to various occasions like weddings, birthdays, and corporate events[1][2][3]. To get the most accurate and up-to-date contact numbers, you can use local business directories like **Justdial**, which provides phone numbers, addresses, and customer ratings for printers across the city[1][4][2].

### Popular Invitation Card Printers in Hyderabad
Below are some of the well-known printing establishments in Hyderabad often listed in local directories:

*   **Ashok Cards** (Secunderabad) – Known for a wide variety of cards and custom designs[1][3].
*   **Maheshwari Printing & Foiling Works** (Lakdi-ka-pul) – Highly rated for printing and foiling services[1].
*   **Sri Shubhalekha Cards** (Ameerpet) – Popular for wedding invitation printing[1].
*   **King Digital Printing** (Madhapur) – Offers quick digital printing services[1].
*   **New Crystal Cards** (Chatta Bazar, Old City) – Known for a wide range of affordable to premium cards; contact: **9100500240**[5].
*   **Fine Digital Prints** (Narayanguda) – Specializes in customized invitation printing; contact: **08047673067**[6].
*   **N and V Invitations** (Jubilee Hills) – Known for professional wedding invitation printing[7].

### How to Find Contact Information
Since phone numbers for local businesses can change, the best way to get the current contact details is:

1.  **Visit [Justdial.com](https://www.justdial.com):** Search for "Wedding Card Printers in Hyderabad"[4]. This will give you a list of shops, their specific locations (e.g., Ameerpet, Secunderabad, Madhapur), and a **"Call Now"** button to connect directly with them[1][4].
2.  **Google Maps:** You can search for "invitation card printing near me" in Google Maps. This will show you the shops closest to your location, their contact numbers, and cus

f you have a specific design or bulk requirement[1][5][7]. Always ask if they provide sample orders so you can check the print quality before finalizing your order[7].

*   **Lakdi-ka-pul / Khairatabad:** Offers a variety of digital and offset printing options[1][2].

### How to Find Contact Details
Because there are over a thousand printers in Hyderabad[4], the most effective way to get current phone numbers and addresses is through local business directories:

1.  **Justdial:** Search for ["Wedding Card Printers in Hyderabad"](https://www.justdial.com) on their website or app[1][4]. They provide direct "Call Now" buttons, verified addresses, and customer reviews to help you compare options[1][4][5].
2.  **Google Maps:** Search for "Wedding card printers near me" while in Hyderabad. This will show you the closest shops with their current contact information, opening hours, and photos of their work.

### Examples of Well-Known Printers
While you should verify their current details via the links above, some frequently mentioned names in Hyderabad include:
*   **Ashok Cards** (Secunderabad)[1][4]
*   **Batcha Cards** (Gowliguda)[3][6]
*   **Sree Digitals** (Lingampally)[4]
*   **Arrow Paper Products** (Nampally)[4]
*   **Subhalekha Cards**[6]

**Tip:** When contacting them, it is often helpful to ask if they offer **in-house design consultation**[1][3] and what their **turnaround time** is for printing, especially if you have a specific deadline.

5: document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
6:   // 1. DataLayer CTA Tracking
7:   // Attaches event listeners to buttons with data-track attribute
8:   const trackableElements = document.querySelectorAll('[data-track]');
9:   
10:   trackableElements.forEach(el => {
11:     el.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
12:       if (typeof gtag !== 'undefined') {
13:         gtag('event', el.getAttribute('data-track'), {
14:           'button_text': el.innerText.trim(),
15:           'page': window.location.pathname,
16:           'section': el.closest('section')?.id || 'header'
17:         });
18:       }
19:     });
20:   });
21: 
22:   // 2. Pricing Visibility Tracking
23:   // Triggers an event when the pricing section enters the viewport
24:   const pricingSection = document.getElementById('pricing');
25:   if (pricingSection && typeof IntersectionObserver !== 'undefined') {
26:     const observer = new IntersectionObserver((entries) => {
27:       entries.forEach(entry => {
28:         if (entry.isIntersecting) {
29:           if (typeof gtag !== 'undefined') {
30:             gtag('event', 'pricing_viewed', {
31:               'page': window.location.pathname,
32:               'segment': document.body.getAttribute('data-segment') || 'individuals'
33:             });
34:           }
35:           observer.disconnect(); // Only fire once per page load
36:         }
37:       });
38:     }, { threshold: 0.5 });
39:     
40:     observer.observe(pricingSection);
41:   }
42: 
43:   // 3. Smooth scrolling for anchor links
44:   document.querySelectorAll('a[href^="#"]').forEach(anchor => {
45:     anchor.addEventListener('click', function (e) {
46:       const targetId = this.getAttribute('href');
47:       if (targetId === '#') return;
48:       
49:       const targetElement = document.querySelector(targetId);
50:       if (targetElement) {
51:         e.preventDefault();
52:         targetElement.scrollIntoView({
53:           behavior: 'smooth'
54:         });
55:       }
56:     });
57:   });
58: 
59:   // 4. FAQ Accordion Toggle
60:   document.querySelectorAll('.faq-question').forEach(button => {
61:     button.addEventListener('click', () => {
62:       const item = button.closest('.faq-item');
63:       const isActive = item.classList.contains('active');
64:       
65:       // Close all other FAQ items
66:       document.querySelectorAll('.faq-item.active').forEach(openItem => {

68:       });
69:       
70:       // Toggle current item
